# Project status

## October 14 — verify live routes after Pages deployment

**Question:** can a successful static build and artifact upload be followed by a repeatable check of what the public site actually serves?

**Change:** added `scripts/check_live_site.py` to verify the deployed HTTPS homepage, Field Companion and resource directory each return HTML with a unique title, description, self-canonical and `index,follow`; it also checks that the live XML sitemap contains those routes and that live `robots.txt` names the sitemap without blocking all crawlers. The manual Pages workflow now checks out the public source with persisted credentials disabled, deploys only the already-checked artifact, then runs this smoke check with bounded retries.

**Validation:** six focused verifier tests cover a valid deployment, retry after propagation delay, stale noindex metadata, HTTPS-only URLs, standalone script execution and workflow ordering. All 140 repository Python tests and 109 Node tests passed. The explicit-origin public candidate produced 110 pages plus 404; `scripts/check_site.py` passed with 111 HTML pages, 5,534 checked links and all 110 public pages reachable. The verifier also passed against the current live site with certificate verification enabled: all three routes had expected metadata, the live sitemap contained 110 URLs, and robots named the sitemap.

**Limit:** this confirms HTTP delivery and crawl configuration, not Google Search Console processing, indexing or ranking. Search Console's sitemap-fetch report remains unresolved. The post-deploy workflow step itself will be exercised by the next authorized Pages deployment.

## October 14 — full review of parrot touchscreen ergonomics

**Question:** what does the CHI 2024 parrot touchscreen paper support for Talk2Nature's mobile product, and what remains a separate animal-computer-interaction question?

**Evidence reviewed:** read the full published article text, Sections 1–7, from the University of Glasgow record and PDF. The study enrolled 20 adult home-kept parrots and 17 completed its trained tablet task. On its 10.1-inch Galaxy Tab A setup, hit rate increased from 19.29% at 40 dp to 47.08% at 130 dp; target distance was not significant, and touch-point precision did not significantly differ by width. The paper's caregiver survey/pilot, participant screening, welfare procedures, task, analysis and limitations were reviewed. Figures were not visually reanalyzed; no raw data, code, supplements or independent analysis were inspected.

**Product and rights decision:** this is tablet ergonomics evidence, not a phone specification, communication test or justification for automated playback. Keep the current Field Companion human-operated and passive. Any parrot-operated enrichment interface is a separate product/welfare project requiring qualified ACI and avian review. The repository lists CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 and “Data Availability Statement: No”; the catalog adds only an original summary and metadata-only paper record, with no article files or study data.

## October 14 — add a published counterinterpretation and check the 2022 outcome tables

**Question:** what does the Japanese-tit source-linkage experiment support, and does it uniquely establish Core-Merge or hierarchical syntax?

**Evidence reviewed:** added Beckers et al. (2024), *No evidence for language syntax in songbird vocalizations* (Frontiers in Psychology 15:1393895; source 318). Read Sections 1–4 of its open full text through PubMed Central and compared its interpretation with the already-reviewed 2016, 2017 and 2022 primary studies and Schlenker et al. (2024). The critique agrees the experiments show receiver sensitivity to call order and different responses to one-source versus two-source AR, but disputes the Core-Merge/hierarchical-syntax inference. It offers finite-list and finite-state/concatenation accounts and flags the 2017 cross-species sequence as a result a unified account must explain. This is a published conceptual critique, not a new experiment or a consensus statement.

**Data check:** rechecked the official Figshare v2 API for item 18007046 v1. It currently returns CC BY 4.0 and per-file MD5 values for the two 2,058-byte tables and two R scripts. The locally retained files match those sizes and MD5s; this supersedes the October 13 snapshot that reported null provider checksums. Added a standard-library descriptive audit and synthetic tests. It verified the two 64-row tables align on all flock-trial metadata and agree on whether each trial has any outcome event. For the binary table, the natural one-source 1A-R treatment had approach in 13/16 flock trials and wing-flicking in 15/16; the other treatments had 2–3/16 and 1–4/16 respectively. The aggregate report emits no rows, dates, caller IDs, locations or audio. It does not fit the deposited mixed-effects models, reproduce adjusted contrasts, or identify a communication mechanism.

**Change and limits:** updated the Japanese-tit source note and reviewer packet, added a separate metadata-only paper resource, and recorded the aggregate report at `research/japanese_tit_2022_descriptives.json` with its script at `research/japanese_tit_2022_descriptives.py`. No original files or third-party code were added to Git, no model was trained, and no external reviewer was contacted. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**; the full independent review and inferential reproduction gates remain open.

## October 14 — expand the Japanese-tit reviewer packet with an adjacent receiver study

**Question:** does the 2025 Carolina-wren/great-tit playback add evidence about cross-family receiver responses, and can it support claims about call-order decoding?

**Evidence reviewed:** source 268 was rechecked against the publisher-indexed Methods, Results and Discussion passages and the University of Western Australia repository abstract, publication record and article-license metadata. Direct ScienceDirect retrieval returned HTTP 403; full article files, figures, supplementary material and complete statistical details were not reviewed.

**Finding and limits:** the study used 96 wild great tits across six treatments. Approach differed between calls and controls but not among call treatments; wing-flicking was more frequent after complete FME–D sequences than after D-only calls or controls. No reversed-order condition was tested, so the result is adjacent cross-family receiver-response evidence, not evidence of Japanese-tit order decoding or an independent replication. The authors note that the Carolina-wren component functions remain unconfirmed and that the D-only comparison was not duty-cycle matched; the stimulus set also reduces four callers to two constructed tracks per call treatment.

**Change and rights:** added source 268 to the Japanese-tit evidence note as a separate comparator, expanded the five-question review request and updated `docs/JAPANESE_TIT_REVIEW.md` with the study and an internal link to its dedicated note. The UWA record identifies the article as CC BY; this does not license its separate Macaulay Library or Xeno-Canto recordings. No article file, audio, data or code was acquired. No reviewer was contacted, and no study, model or animal-facing protocol is proposed.

**Validation:** all 125 repository Python tests passed; the catalog accepted 130 resources. The owner-only metadata catalog synced 512 records (two changed, none created or retired) and an immediate repeat made no changes. The local noindex build produced 107 pages plus 404; the checker validated 108 HTML pages and 5,257 links. The explicit-origin public candidate also produced 107 pages plus 404; the checker validated 108 HTML pages, 5,366 links and all 107 public pages reachable. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Publication receipt (October 14):** PR [#144](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/144) was squash-merged to `main` as [`dece125`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/dece1259dc677ad26f6480b0873c6e53fc7eebf0) after public checks [#38495691153](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38495691153) succeeded. The manual Pages workflow [#38495836412](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38495836412) completed successfully from that commit. Live HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for `/research/bird-call-syntax/`; the page serves its title, description, `index,follow` and self-canonical URL, and the sitemap includes the route. This verifies publication and crawl metadata, not Search Console indexing, impressions or rankings. No independent review or analysis reproduction is claimed.

## October 14 CowRumIA mobile-audio app and evaluation review

**Question:** is CowRumIA a useful mobile app and data comparator for Talk2Nature, and does its reported 85.6% result support an independent performance claim?

**Evidence reviewed:** pinned the GitHub default-branch snapshot to 44fdc5e5cf3f080457211bb87eb8cb2fc6b099ac; read its README and MIT LICENSE; statically reviewed model/train_model.py around the split, validation callbacks and final evaluation; and checked the Zenodo v1.0.0 record, file listing and Rights section. New sources 306–308 and three separate resource records distinguish code, model assets and dataset.

**Finding:** the README describes a Flutter Android app and a 624-WAV, four-class cattle corpus. It reports 85.6% on 125 test audios. The pinned script creates a random stratified utterance split, passes the same test subset to model.fit as validation data, uses its validation metrics for early stopping and learning-rate scheduling, and then evaluates on that same subset. The score is therefore not an untouched final-test estimate; the direction or size of bias is unknown. The four labels combine beef/dairy type with stress/calm and have counts from 39 to 350. The reviewed materials do not establish animal/session/site holdout, the annotation protocol, inter-rater agreement or an independent welfare reference.

**Rights and product decision:** GitHub's pinned LICENSE declares MIT for repository software; Zenodo displays a copyright notice but no explicit dataset reuse license. The README-listed TFLite weights and upstream recording rights are separate and unresolved. Keep audio and weights metadata-only, outside the training path. Treat the app as a mobile implementation comparator, not a welfare diagnostic, first-species recommendation or communication result. Add the evidence note to the Models & evaluation learning path. The README setup command's model/cow_rumia_train_v2.py path returned 404 at the pinned commit; no code, app, data or weights were executed or acquired.

**Validation:** all 125 repository Python tests passed; python3 -m talk2nature.collection validate accepted 130 resources. Local noindex build and explicit-origin public candidate each generated 107 pages plus 404. scripts/check_site.py passed on the public candidate with 108 HTML pages, 5,363 links and all 107 public pages reachable. The CowRumIA page has a unique title/description, index,follow, a self-canonical URL and a sitemap entry. The owner-only SQLite metadata catalog synced 512 records (38 created, 23 changed, none retired); an immediate repeat made no changes. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Publication receipt (October 14):** PR [#142](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/142) was squash-merged to `main` as [`e7e47b2`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/e7e47b2782af7453489bf30056575e8b5956cbc3) after public checks [#38494352873](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38494352873) succeeded. The manual Pages workflow [#38494598985](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38494598985) completed successfully from that commit. Live HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for `/research/cowrumia-bovine-audio-app-2026/`; its title, description, `index,follow`, self-canonical URL and sitemap entry were verified. This establishes publication and crawl configuration, not Search Console indexing, impressions or ranking. No data, model weights or upstream code were acquired or run; no independent reproduction or specialist review is claimed.

## October 14: budgerigar dataset-alignment correction and evidence refresh

**Question:** does the public Dryad deposit provide synchronized vocal events and observed social behavior, and did the earlier summary describe the recorded social group correctly?

**Evidence reviewed:** the [primary article](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10265024/) Methods on animal housing, experimental outline, audio and video recording; the [official Dryad version history, file inventory and README](https://datadryad.org/dataset/doi:10.5061/dryad.fttdz08xr); and Dryad's [reuse policy](https://datadryad.org/help/guides/reuse). The Dryad README describes the interobserver workbook and its recoded subset. The workbook itself was not acquired or inspected.

**Correction and finding:** an October 11 note incorrectly called the videos “home-flock” recordings. The paper describes separate video sessions of the newly formed experimental flocks. Contact calls were collected from an isolated focal bird in separate 55-minute sessions, with visual but not acoustic contact to flockmates; social videos were two 80-minute sessions per flock per recording block. The deposit lists derived call-feature tables and social/proximity coding with overlapping group/date/block fields, but no source call WAVs or shared call/event timestamps. It is a protocol/schema near-match, not an event-level sound-to-behavior benchmark. The README documents an interobserver recoding subset; no reliability value was calculated, and none is claimed. The paper reports age-bounded differences in vocal diversity and affiliative ties while measured plasticity/convergence were similar; these findings do not establish call meaning.

**Rights and decision:** Dryad's policy states data deposits use CC0. The article separately declares CC BY 4.0; the included R scripts have no separately verified software license. No files were downloaded, no code was run, and the resource remains metadata-only. Corrected `content/research.json`, `content/sources.json`, `research/resources.json`, `docs/COLLECTION.md`, `docs/MODEL_DATA_DECISION.md` and `docs/RESEARCH_BACKLOG.md`; added a page-scope regression in `tests/test_site.py`. Next: prioritize a resource with source recordings and independently timed behavior labels; any aggregate analysis of this deposit would require an explicit unit, exact join/missingness audit and qualified review.

**Validation:** all 123 repository Python tests passed; the owner-workbench suite passed 104 tests, with 49 PostgreSQL cases skipped because no disposable test database was configured. The resource validator accepts all 127 records. The local noindex build produced 106 pages plus 404, and the site checker passed on 107 HTML pages and 5,201 links. A public-origin candidate built against the confirmed GitHub Pages URL and passed with 107 HTML pages, 5,308 links and all 106 public pages reachable. The updated note's title, description, `index,follow`, canonical, source links and sitemap entry were verified in generated HTML. The public-origin build is a local candidate; no deployment or live Search Console/indexing result is claimed. The private metadata catalog ends at 505 records; the note edit updated one record, and a repeat sync changed none.

## October 14 funding-route audit — BiodivFuture and UN KBF

**Question:** is there a credible near-term funding route through the open BiodivFuture call or a United Nations biodiversity fund?

**BiodivFuture:** rechecked the official Biodiversa+ call page and 20-page Announcement of Opportunity. Both set the mandatory five-page EPSS pre-proposal deadline at November 10, 2026, 12:00 CET (11:00 UTC). The European Commission portal still lists November 2, so the public opportunity record now preserves the disagreement rather than silently choosing a date. Full-proposal entry requires a submitted pre-proposal. Eligibility requires a transnational ecology-led consortium; the project's host, field study and consortium are not confirmed. No contact or application was made.

**Kunming Biodiversity Fund:** reviewed UNEP's call page and its 13-page official guidelines. Stage 2 closes November 15 at 23:59 on the deadline date, but this is not a direct grant route for Talk2Nature: projects must be country/NBSAP aligned and co-developed with a UN Participating Organization; only UN agencies receive funds directly; non-UN entities may be sub-partners; and a CBD national focal point or Lead Ministry endorsement is required. The project has no confirmed eligible country program, UN partner, field evidence or invited work package. Recorded as a conditional partnership lead, not an application recommendation or an existing relationship.

**Decision and limits:** preserve both opportunities in the public catalog with their deadlines, source links, role/eligibility constraints and explicit non-submission status. No outreach, proposal drafting, commitment, eligibility claim or funding request was made.

## October 14 Android saved-session review stability

**Question:** can a person review an already-saved Android session without the app interrupting keyboard or assistive-technology focus? Source inspection found that the foreground screen polls session state every two seconds and rebuilt the stopped-session event and note list on each refresh. Replacing those child views could interrupt review actions; this is a code-path finding, not user-study evidence.

**Change:** the event list now keeps its existing views while serialized events and notes are unchanged. It rerenders when an event or note changes and clears its snapshot when a session is deleted. A focused core regression check covers the unchanged, changed and reset states. No recording, microphone permission, participant data or external media was used.

**Validation:** the Android core suite and both browser importer/schedule tests pass; the full repository suite passes 121 Python and 109 Node tests. The local noindex build and site checker pass with 106 public pages, 107 HTML files and 5,176 checked links. ## October 13 fungal electrophysiology source and rights follow-up

**Question:** what did the 2026 oyster-fungus propagation paper measure, and can its linked data be safely reused or reproduced?

**Finding:** the primary paper reports approximately 15 days of 1 Hz recording from eight spatially ordered differential channels on one *Pleurotus ostreatus*-colonised substrate. Its lead–lag analysis is evidence reported by the paper for slow electrical propagation consistent with ionic waves. It is not a receiver-response experiment or evidence of decoded language. The eight channels are spatial subsamples of one specimen, and the Methods do not describe external stimulation or a sham/uncolonised-substrate comparison.

**Provenance and rights:** the paper cites Zenodo DOI [10.5281/zenodo.4430968](https://zenodo.org/records/4430968). The official record is v1, created in 2021, lists one 413.2 MB ZIP and provider MD5 `ae00e6ace4114f8ca33572b4930c41b4`, and displays no dataset license. The record does not identify which archive member contains the paper's 15-day, eight-channel series. The article and dataset now have separate resource records; the UWE repository identifies the paper as CC BY 4.0, which does not grant rights to the dataset.

**Change and limits:** updated the fungal research note and added the separate article/data records. No archive was downloaded, no checksum was computed locally, no data were analyzed, and no independent specialist reviewed the paper. Before any reproduction, resolve the file mapping and terms, then obtain mycology/electrophysiology review of independent biological replication, controls and environmental logging. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY** for this or any other biological benchmark.

**Publication receipt (October 13):** [PR #118](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/118) merged as `1fa30b8`; the [manual Pages workflow](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38428567366) completed successfully. Live fetches confirmed HTTP 200 for the [fungal evidence page](https://metivity.github.io/talk2nature/research/fungal-propagation-2026/) and [resource directory](https://metivity.github.io/talk2nature/research/resources/); the new title and source links are served, the separate paper/data records appear with the dataset marked metadata-only, and the route is in the sitemap named by `robots.txt`. This verifies publication and crawl configuration, not Google indexing, impressions or ranking; Search Console was not inspected.

## October 14 fungal-potential-card full-text review

**Question:** what did the fungal-potential-card study actually establish, and do its linked dataset and analysis code qualify for reuse?

**Finding:** full PMC article text and Table 1 report one *Fusarium oxysporum* strain (NEU 195) and a significant pre-induction PSD difference of about 1,604.752% between 24 fungal and 8 non-inoculated electrode-pair records (p<0.001). The main text reviewed does not establish that electrode-pair records are independent biological replicates. The post-induction drug/carrier comparisons each report n=3 and are nonsignificant (p=.327, .391, .580 and .116). DMSO reproduces the calcimycin pattern and is considered a likely electrode confound; the sodium-azide series has a water-control anomaly. This is specialized extracellular electrophysiology, not evidence of a fungal message or semantic code.

**Rights and change:** updated the existing research note, source records and separate paper, dataset and code resources. The [Mendeley v1 record](https://data.mendeley.com/datasets/srkxbkh6sp/1) displays CC BY 4.0, but its visible Files section lists no file entries. The article identifies supplementary Method S1; its bytes and component-specific license remain unreviewed. No dataset, supplement or code was downloaded or executed, and no result was reproduced. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Next gate:** resolve the exact dataset manifest and independent-culture structure; review the supplement and reconcile its Method S1 data-statement pointer with the software table's Document S2 code reference and terms. Obtain mycology/electrophysiology review before any acquisition or reproduction. The evidence page remains an original summary with canonical metadata, sitemap inclusion and crawlability checks; this does not establish search indexing or ranking.

## October 13 Android Station capture-deadline hardening

**Question:** does the Android prototype's selected 1-, 4- or 8-hour cap still get checked if the microphone stops delivering audio frames?

**Finding and change:** the capture loop previously used a blocking `AudioRecord.read()` and compared the limit only with processed audio duration. A stalled read could therefore postpone the app's next time-limit check. Added a monotonic `SystemClock.elapsedRealtime()` deadline, bounded each audio read to samples fitting before that deadline, and switched to non-blocking reads with a short idle wait. The [Android clock reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/os/SystemClock) documents that `elapsedRealtime()` is monotonic and includes sleep; the [AudioRecord reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/media/AudioRecord) documents that `READ_NON_BLOCKING` returns immediately and may return zero samples. Added deterministic deadline arithmetic and eight-hour maximum tests. This is a source-level safety improvement, not proof of an exact stop time on every device.

**Validation:** Android core checks pass; all 107 Node tests and 114 Python tests pass; `git diff --check` passes. This host has no Android SDK, emulator or `adb`, so Gradle package/lint checks and physical-device behavior are not locally verified; the repository pull-request workflow runs Android lint and debug/release assembly. No microphone, recording, deployment or research data was used.

**Release:** PR [#102](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/102) merged as [`7552b6d`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/7552b6d2ee7247a689d4d239b9b60eb467ab4709). The public-checks workflow passed, including Android lint, debug/release assembly, release-boundary checks and the site build/check. No Pages deployment occurred because the change is Android-only.

**Next:** test limit timing, screen lock, calls and interruption on a named physical device before describing this as field-ready. The broad evidence, app, open-source, SEO and resources goal remains active.

## October 13 NLnet open-technology funding screen

Checked the current official [Restack call](https://nlnet.nl/restack/), [CodeSupply call](https://nlnet.nl/codesupply/), [CodeSupply eligibility](https://nlnet.nl/codesupply/eligibility/), [NLnet GenAI policy](https://nlnet.nl/foundation/policies/generativeAI/), [proposal form](https://nlnet.nl/propose/) and [September call notice](https://nlnet.nl/news/2026/20260903-call.html). Both deadlines are November 3, 2026 at 12:00 CET, with stated first-proposal amounts of €5,000–€50,000. Restack is out of current scope: AI-related work is excluded unless it already has more than one million active human users and direct open-internet-stack relevance. CodeSupply is conditional at best: it concerns reusable software-supply-chain metadata; Talk2Nature currently catalogs research resources and has no established package-metadata need or deliverable. Its eligibility page calls for a strong European dimension and describes the usual 60% funding route for team members in Horizon Europe-eligible countries; our applicant/team facts are unconfirmed. The September notice advises LLM-using applicants to wait for a revised GenAI policy. The current policy/form also make proposal provenance, AI-use disclosure and human verification material readiness conditions. No application was drafted or submitted and no organization was contacted. Reassess CodeSupply only if a concrete scope-aligned package-metadata contribution, eligibility facts and the applicable AI policy are established; otherwise leave it unpursued.

## October 13 common-endophyte network evidence note

**Question:** does a recent plant–fungus experiment change what Talk2Nature can responsibly say about communication through fungal networks? **Evidence reviewed:** Waschk et al., *Evidence for resource transfer via common endophyte networks*, Scientific Reports 16, article 26111 (2026), including its full article, methods, limitations, data-availability and CC BY 4.0 rights statement. The paper reports a split-plate Arabidopsis experiment with three endophytes and stable-isotope measurements. Among its reported outcomes, *Fusarium temperatum* treatments showed 55% higher amino-acid-derived 15N in receiver plants and 38% higher biomass than controls; *Trichoderma viride* showed a smaller 13C enrichment (+2.83%). The study measures resource and physiological outcomes, not a sender message, receiver interpretation or communication.

**Change and rights:** added an original note, two source records, separate paper/data/code catalog entries, a multimodal learning-path link and a regression that preserves the communication and rights boundaries. The official GitLab API pins the current `main` tree to commit `5b4ea51c038213e62feb6580daa5a48ccbfa32ff` (2025-12-04) and lists `CEN.csv` (12,643 bytes), `isotope_analysis.R` (35,976 bytes) and `physiology_analysis.R` (13,080 bytes). The project API declares no license and the inspected root contains no README or license file. The paper is CC BY 4.0; dataset and code rights are separately unresolved. No dataset, code, paper asset or supplement was downloaded, executed or redistributed. No field location was mapped because the study reports no field collection or study-site location.

**Verification and limits:** 105 Python tests and 107 Node tests passed; the catalog validates 101 resources; `git diff --check` passed. The public-origin local build produced 101 pages plus 404; the site checker verified 102 HTML documents, 4,944 links and 101 reachable public pages. The note has its own title, description, self-canonical, `index,follow` and local sitemap entry. Live HTTPS checks still return 404 for this page; the deployed sitemap has 99 URLs. No deployment occurred, and no Search Console indexing evidence was obtained. This controlled plant/fungus study is not a nature-audio benchmark or app capability. Next: obtain independent plant–fungus methods review and establish data/code terms before considering reproduction. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 13 cross-family mobbing-response study

Added a source-grounded research note and separate paper resource for Coye & Dutour (2025), plus a coarse Lyon-region marker and a link from the “meaning” learning path. The publisher-indexed article text reports 96 wild great tits across six playback treatments, one exposure per bird. Call playbacks increased approach relative to controls, without significant differences among call treatments; sequence playbacks elicited more wing-flicking than D-call or control playbacks. The paper labels its complete two-part sequence FME–D and says the Carolina-wren components' alert/recruitment functions were unconfirmed. The Carolina-wren versus great-tit sequence contrast was nonsignificant and underpowered, not evidence of equivalence. No reversed Carolina-wren sequence was included, and only two constructed tracks were used for each Carolina-wren call treatment. Direct ScienceDirect retrieval returned HTTP 403; the institutional repository supplied abstract and CC BY 4.0 metadata. No article file, audio, dataset, code or independent review was acquired. The note records limits on semantic interpretation and directs the next review to the full paper and data availability. This local change is not deployed; AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 13 Horizon Europe call fit and status check

**Question:** does the Horizon Europe HORIZON-NATURE-2027-02-05 call offer a credible funding path for Talk2Nature, and is it confirmed open today?

**Evidence reviewed:** the official REA call schedule and general applicant guidance, plus pages 13–15 and 144–147 of the official Horizon Europe Work Programme 2026–2027 Part 14. The work programme schedules opening on October 13, 2026 and a deadline of January 14, 2027 at 17:00 Brussels time. It allocates €16 million to the topic and expects three projects at €5–6 million each, as a lump-sum research-and-innovation action. The scope is policy-specific: the EU Pollinators Monitoring Scheme, soil biodiversity under the Soil Monitoring Law, or Water Framework Directive monitoring. It expects scalable, cost-effective methods, demonstrations, species identification/quantification, relevant DNA libraries/genomic methods, and FAIR-by-design data. General REA guidance says most calls require at least three organisations from three different EU or associated countries, including one EU member; call-specific portal terms control. On October 13 the direct Funding & Tenders topic URL returned HTTP 200 with a generic JavaScript shell, but no topic record, submission state or application form was exposed in the direct response; browser-rendered status remains unverified.

**Decision:** no current application fit. Talk2Nature has an experimental passive audio observation/annotation workflow, not a validated statutory monitoring method, species-counting system, DNA workflow or Water Framework Directive tool; it has no confirmed scientific lead, applicant entity or eligible consortium. A bounded software or interoperability contribution could be considered only if a qualified consortium demonstrates a need and the workflow is tested for that use. The official Funding & Tenders topic page could not be retrieved during this check, so the actual submission status is unverified despite the scheduled opening date. Do not label the opportunity open until the live portal confirms it. No registration, outreach, expense or application was made.

**Repository record and limits:** refreshed the opportunity record and source review dates, adding source 240 for the official REA schedule and source 260 for the exact Funding & Tenders portal route. The public opportunity card now links directly to the live topic separately from the work-programme PDF, with submission status explicitly unverified. No application has been drafted or submitted and no eligibility is claimed. The October 16 information day and October 19 brokerage event are discovery options; no registration or partner contact occurred. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**. At the time of this candidate note the change was not deployed; PR #85 later released it, as recorded below.

## October 13 Wildlife Acoustics product-grant go/no-go

**Question:** could the Q4 Wildlife Acoustics grant responsibly support a Talk2Nature field tool, and what must be true before an application is worth preparing?

**Official-page check:** a fresh direct HTTPS fetch returned HTTP 200. The current program page lists a November 15, 2026 deadline and awards by December 30; it asks applicants to apply at least one grant quarter ahead of when equipment is needed. Standard awards are products, up to $4,000 per applicant from a quarterly product pool up to $12,000. Software-only awards are annual Kaleidoscope Pro licenses. Applications must be in English and include the organization's charitable/educational tax-exempt status document; only the recommendation-letter requirement is waived for software-only applications. The page also says recipient projects may be featured on the company's website/social media and notes applicable taxes may be due where tax-exempt status is unavailable. Source 213 was refreshed to record this exact review scope.

**Decision:** no current application. These are in-kind research products, not cash for general application development. Talk2Nature has no confirmed eligible host, wild-species field study or qualified scientific lead, and therefore no validated product requirement to request. Reconsider only if an eligible educational/charitable host and qualified lead define a passive, welfare-reviewed study, identify a needed eligible product and verify applicant-specific documents/tax terms. No portal login, application, outreach or financial commitment occurred. This is a conditional opportunity, not a claim that Talk2Nature or Metivity is eligible.

## October 13: companion-parrot survey review and privacy boundary

Reviewed the full main text of Benedict et al. (2022) through Europe PMC XML and refreshed official Figshare API metadata for dataset v4. The paper describes 898 voluntary responses, of which 877 parrot entries across 73 species remained after quality checks and removing birds not reported to mimic human sounds. Caregivers reported contextual use without human prompting, but this was not independently observed or tested with receivers. Recruitment was through email/social media and parrot groups. The paper also says the questionnaire requested respondent names and email.

Added the dataset as resource `companion-parrot-mimicry-survey-data-2022`, metadata-only. Figshare declares item-level CC BY 4.0 and lists one 186,629-byte XLSX, without an API-reported MD5. The workbook and supplementary questionnaire were not acquired or inspected; identifiers, consent scope, and privacy suitability are unknown. No survey data are admitted for training. This strengthens the public resource library and a clear app-design lesson: separate what a caregiver reports from directly observed context, recordings, and later scientific interpretation.

## October 12 AutoResearch integration check

## October 12: cross-species speech-encoder transfer evidence

**Question:** do human-speech encoders carry useful structure into animal vocalizations, and does that show language-like understanding?

**Evidence reviewed:** the complete arXiv v1 HTML and version/license metadata for Arias-Vergara et al., source 218. The study compares 15 frozen encoders on caller identification and call-type classification across four species. It reports speech-pretrained representations significantly ahead in three of seven comparisons, with no significant difference in four; multilingual and animal-domain pretraining show no systematic advantage. Crucially, some caller-ID folds share individuals across partitions because a session was divided into blocks. The session-independent sensitivity results are lower. The manuscript license is CC BY 4.0; source corpora, code and model weights have separate rights.

**Change:** added an original indexable note, a metadata-only paper resource, a source record, and a link in the model-transfer learning path. The atlas location status remains pending because the reused corpora need original-source location review. No files were copied or acquired, no code or models were run, and no author was contacted.

**Checks and release:** 90 Python tests and 98 Node tests pass; the resource validator accepts 62 records. The public build generates 90 pages plus 404; the site checker verifies 91 HTML files, 4,228 links and 90 reachable public pages. The live note, resource directory and sitemap returned HTTP 200; the note has its expected canonical and `index,follow`, and the sitemap lists 90 URLs including the note. PR [#53](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/53) merged as `319942e86ee270f26d1678a267296e6ebc32536b` and deployed through successful [Pages run #38299565431](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38299565431).

**Limits and next decision:** this arXiv record does not establish peer review, and the study tests acoustic labels, not independently observed context or message meaning. A bounded exact-title/ID search on GitHub, Zenodo and OpenReview returned no official code/data or venue match; it does not prove none exists. Independent reproduction and specialist review remain absent; AutoResearch is **NOT READY**. Keep the record metadata-only unless an official release or concrete reproduction need emerges; then have a bioacoustic-methods reviewer assess the caller split before considering a bounded run. Search Console indexing was not refreshed after deployment.

## October 2: media/model lineage continuation

Extended the private foundation to schema v2: immutable synthetic audio/video descriptors, clock alignment, frozen model specifications and inputs, and invented result records. The graph now connects sources → frozen study → session → observation → media/alignment → reviewed release → model specification → result. Exact API and migration boundaries are in `docs/MEDIA_LINEAGE.md`. The owner graph uses readable relationship names; these are API-backed research records, not a new consumer upload feature.

All new routes preserve exact owner authorization, CSRF, bounded JSON and synthetic-only admission. Media and alignment membership freeze at observation review. Model specifications require reviewed training permission, matching release/media fingerprints, complete grouped splits and explicit alignment for every multimodal input. Outputs retain probabilities/abstention/calibration separately from human labels and must fit the relevant validity interval. No bytes, object URL, upload grant, playback, model execution or real biological result was added.

Withdrawal redacts media/alignment metadata, invalidates all dependent runs, redacts their results even on other inputs, revokes releases and removes the observation in one transaction. Failure injection verifies full rollback. Opaque provenance tombstones remain private for reconciliation. SQLite and PostgreSQL migrations preserve existing rows; runtime does not silently migrate an old schema. Recovery understands both table inventories and includes lineage tombstones.

Validation: 104 backend tests passed across both database adapters with no skips, plus 36 Python repository tests and 44 Node tests. Local/public builds passed (51 HTML pages, 1,638 / 1,690 links), and the private UI script passed syntax checking. New tests exercise auth/CSRF, real-data rejection, hash/modality/session boundaries, interval/uncertainty bounds, frozen membership, grouped splits, exact multimodal coverage, label separation, restart, tamper detection, cross-input withdrawal and rollback. SQLite migration failure rolls back DDL/version; PostgreSQL migration retains owner/data and denies runtime DDL.

The actual local database was backed up to ignored `data/private/recovery/pre-lineage-v2-20261002/` and explicitly migrated to v2. Owner identity and all 131 public catalog records were compared with the backup and preserved; foreign-key checks passed. Its graph still has 131 nodes / 58 links, with no synthetic study/media fixtures added to the owner's data. Ignored `tmp/discovery/lineage-local-migration.json` records the non-sensitive result. An initial verification command attempted nested SQLite transactions and timed out after the migration; rerunning verification in separate transactions passed. No restore was activated.

The public website remains the verified `06c75ab` Pages deployment. This continuation changes private source and repository documentation only. It walks a contributor through making an invented two-tone sample, marking two timed events with Unicode and escaped-newline note cases, importing the WAV and labels in Audacity, and comparing the checksums and expected spans. It records the Audacity 4.0.1 macOS arm64 import as one tested configuration, offers a privacy-safe compatibility issue form, and states that this is only a file-format check—not animal communication, interpretation, or model validation.

Desktop and narrow-layout previews were inspected. All 49 Python tests, 77 Node tests, local build, 70-page/2,849-link site check and whitespace check passed. The lab is in the local `codex/private-research-foundation` working tree and local noindex preview only; it has not been pushed or deployed. Independent researcher reproduction and cross-platform compatibility remain open.

## October 7: parrot prototype review and field visit

Raviv identified a parrot app in Downloads. The likely match calls itself KeeKee (two e's); it was reviewed statically in place. The full private notes are in ignored `funding/keekee-static-review.md`. Strong product ideas include a direct parrot entry, a local clip diary and several understandable tasks. The workspace has no top-level source license or notice, and its bundled sound licenses were not independently verified. Its live mode can automatically play calls and turns a later sound inside a short timer into an adaptive reward. The prompt distinguishes calls, mimicry, nearby human voices and visual context. No microphone starts until the user confirms permission and presses Start. The new preset code is in the exact offline-shell allowlist; the service worker accepts only the explicit `mode=parrot` query.

Validation: 79 Node tests and 50 Python tests passed; the local noindex build produced 70 pages plus 404 and the checker verified 71 HTML pages / 2,886 links. Its detail page said “Sitemap could not be read” and showed Last read October 11. A live URL Inspection of the exact sitemap at 7:07 PM reported Crawl allowed = Yes, Page fetch = Successful, Indexing allowed = Yes; the tested response was HTTP 200 with `application/xml`. Its displayed source contained 70 unique absolute URLs, all under the project path, each with `<lastmod>2026-10-04</lastmod>`. This verifies that Google's Inspection tool can currently retrieve the file, but not that the sitemap-processing system accepted it; the report still gives no specific failure reason. Live URL tests at 7:08 and 7:09 PM also confirmed `/tools/interop/` and `/community/university/` are available to Google and indexable. These supersede the earlier audit's 404 assumption; no indexing request was made for either route. The sitemap discrepancy remains open. I made no further sitemap submission or deployment.

Added a public-build invariant to `scripts/check_site.py`: sitemap URLs must be unique and match the canonical URLs of all public HTML pages, excluding the noindex 404 page. ## October 11: Search Console follow-up and sitemap accuracy

Rechecked the exact URL-prefix property while signed in as `raviv@metivity.com`. The submitted `/sitemap.xml` remains Type “Unknown,” Status “Couldn't fetch,” with zero discovered pages/videos and Last read October 11. The Page indexing report still shows its October 4 snapshot: one indexed URL, zero not-indexed URLs and zero impressions; the overview shows zero search clicks. A read-only inspection of `/research/birdnet/` said the URL was unknown to Google and had no referring sitemap. Its separate live test then reported “URL is available to Google” and “Page can be indexed.” No indexing request was made. This points to a discovery/indexing gap while confirming that this representative page is not blocked from crawling; it does not identify the sitemap processor's failure cause.

The public sitemap currently serves 72 unique project URLs, but the live file assigns `2026-10-04` to every `<lastmod>`, including pages edited after that date. The static builder now omits `<lastmod>` rather than claim inaccurate per-page freshness; Google recommends supplying it only when it is consistently accurate and reflects a significant page update ([sitemap guidance](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/crawling-indexing/sitemaps/build-sitemap)). A regression test protects that rule. Validation passed all 53 repository tests; the local public build generated 73 sitemap URLs, zero `<lastmod>` elements, and passed the site checker for 74 HTML pages and 3,130 links. This fix is local and undeployed; it cannot resolve the Search Console discrepancy until a later approved public release is crawled, and sitemap acceptance remains unverified.

### Atlas follow-up: map the study region, preserve the unresolved dates

The MammAlps note now has one coarse Swiss National Park region marker. The authors explicitly withhold the three camera sites, so no site-level point is published. A source check found conflicting dates: arXiv v2 and official CVPR 2025 main text say June–August 2023; the arXiv/CVPR supplement and exact Zenodo v3 record say August–October 2023. Official CVPR search-index excerpts corroborate both sides, but direct paper/supplement fetches returned 403, so the published-version review remains partial. The atlas leaves the observation interval unset and surfaces this conflict in the note. The official park visitor-centre page is only regional context, not a camera-site source. This raises the literature map to 22 mapped notes and 22 approximate regions, now citing 132 source records; it does not create new participant observations or a training dataset. Validation: 52 repository Python tests, 79 Node tests and 104 admin tests passed (49 PostgreSQL integration tests skipped); local/public builds checked 74 HTML pages, with 3,055 local and 3,130 public links; the catalog import added two source records and one evidence version (191 total catalog records), then repeated with zero changes. Generated page metadata, canonical route, sitemap entry and public atlas projection passed checks. Manual browser visual review is pending: browser policy blocked local-file navigation and the localhost URL returned an unrelated site. No live deployment occurred.

## October 11: post-release Search Console verification

After Pages release `7d56893`, Search Console was checked under the verified property. Its overview showed one indexed URL and zero web-search clicks. The new public parrotlet page’s Google Index result says “URL is not on Google,” with “URL is unknown to Google” and no referring sitemap. The sitemap was not resubmitted and the page was not sent an indexing request during this audit. The report has not supplied a code-level failure reason, so no speculative sitemap code change was made. ## October 12 continuation: companion-parrot name-use evidence — local, not deployed

Added an original, source-linked evidence note for Benedict et al. (2026), `Name use by companion parrots`, and its May 2026 correction. The primary PLOS article was reviewed for abstract, methods, results and discussion. It reports survey data from 1,202 companion parrots across 89 species; its Results section reports 801 example phrases containing names while its abstract reports 802. The correction only adds three author ORCID identifiers. The note records this discrepancy rather than silently choosing a value.

The study is useful for designing an observation app that keeps direct observations separate from a caregiver's interpretation. It is not an audio dataset, a controlled test of natural calls or evidence of general translation. Methods say the survey requested respondent and parrot names; the article describes its S2 phrase dataset as de-identified, but that file was not inspected or acquired. The resource remains metadata-only pending file-level privacy and rights review. The new page has canonical, indexable metadata in a public fixture build and is included in the generated sitemap. This is local work: no deployment or Search Console refresh occurred. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 12 continuation: published fruit-bat context-model audit — local, not deployed

Reviewed the complete peer-reviewed EUSIPCO 2024 paper and official arXiv v1 manuscript on context prediction using the Egyptian fruit-bat archive. Added a separate research note, two versioned source records and a paper resource, then linked the note from the model-evaluation reading path. The study reports 35,074 retained video-context-labeled calls, three subject-independent development/test folds, and UAR of 22.4% for a pitch-feature SVM, 27.7% for ResNet-50 and 33.3% for EfficientNet-B0 initialized with ImageNet weights. Its internal train/validation split is stratified random and ignores identity; its sample-bootstrap intervals are not grouped by animal or session; grooming recall is zero. The paper says code would be released upon acceptance, but the targeted official-source and repository search did not locate code or split files. No reproduction or independent validation is claimed.

This refines, rather than clears, the benchmark gate: subject-independent test folds are reported in the paper, while actual membership, whether all caller/addressee animals remain disjoint, and session/date/channel/treatment leakage have not been checked. The next decision is to locate the official code/partition release and obtain independent bat-bioacoustics review before deciding whether `FileInfo.csv` is necessary. A private unsent review brief is in ignored `funding/fruit-bat-review-preparation.md`; no external message was sent. Do not acquire the 31.6 MB file or relax the 5 MiB collector cap without an approved bounded integrity plan. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 12 continuation: screen an open transnational biodiversity call

Reviewed Biodiversa+’s official BiodivFuture 2026–2027 call page, the formal Announcement of Opportunity PDF, the participating-funder rules and the public partner-search page. The call seeks interdisciplinary research into defined novel ecosystems and their biodiversity/socio-ecological trajectories. A first-stage proposal is mandatory: a five-page project description plus budgets by partner, due November 10, 2026 at 12:00 CET (11:00 UTC). Eligibility requires partners from at least three participating countries and funding organisations, including at least two EU Member or Associated Countries.

Israel’s Ministry of Environmental Protection rules list a €120,000 contribution to the call and a €120,000 maximum per project. Beneficiary institutions must be research institutions recognized by the Israel Science Foundation; independent researchers, NGOs and local authorities can collaborate with those institutions and receive funding for their project part. A part of the research must be carried out in Israel. The designated gov.il call page did not return readable text in this review, so further national documents and administrative requirements remain unchecked.

## October 12 continuation: deepen the plant–insect acoustic evidence note — local candidate

Confirmed that the active integrated goal already spans research collection, the phone application, open-source quality, technical SEO and a gated ML benchmark; it remains active, with no duplicate goal created. For this cycle, reviewed the full eLife version-of-record text through NCBI PMC and the official eLife PDF, including the methods, figures, public reviewer comments and author responses. Also checked the official eLife figures/source-data page and Mendeley Data v1 metadata.

The bounded finding is that female *Spodoptera littoralis* oviposition choices changed with playback of drought-stressed tomato-plant clicks, and the preference changed direction when real plants were present. A deafening control removed the plant-free playback preference; a male-moth-click control produced no detected preference. Main choice experiments played 60 clicks/min; a lower 30-click/min long-arena experiment used one moth per arena and a different setup. The authors explicitly describe the plant emissions as likely cues rather than evolved messages. This is one species/plant pair in controlled settings; the reviewer exchange discusses ecological realism, effect presentation, data availability and generalization limits.

Updated the existing evidence note, added source records 186–188 for full text/review, source-data availability and the Mendeley deposit, and added a separate `metadata_only` collection resource. The article states CC BY 4.0; the Mendeley Data v1 record displays CC0 1.0. eLife describes a source-data archive with tables and code, while the Mendeley page describes codes/statistics. No files were downloaded, inventoried, executed or independently reproduced; no raw audio is assumed. The public metadata catalog validates 41 resources and now contains 282 records (4 created, 3 changed across the note, sources and final review-stage correction); the final repeat created or changed none.

## October 12 continuation: reproduce participant structure without releasing animal IDs

**Question.** Can the already approved, small video-derived annotation component inform a leakage review for the 2024 Egyptian-fruit-bat context paper, without training or publishing raw rows?

**Evidence reviewed.** Rechecked the full EUSIPCO paper and exact-title/arXiv/GitHub searches for its promised code and folds; no matching public release surfaced in these bounded searches. Visually reviewed all five pages of the separately acquired Figshare Metadata.pdf (item 4555900; its API gave no upstream MD5, and its local receipt makes no source-checksum claim). Re-ran the receipt- and checksum-verified aggregate inspection of the full Annotations.csv item (4555903). Metadata tables describe 20 date-bounded treatment periods, social composition, recording channels, bat IDs, maternal relationships, and both emitter/addressee annotation fields. Negative role values preserve a known individual with uncertain role; zero means unknown.

**Artifact.** Extended `scripts/inspect_bat_annotations.py` and its synthetic regression test to output a participant-pair graph using both role columns, preserving uncertainty and exporting counts only. Added the reproducible `research/figshare_bat_annotation_report.json`, source records 190–191, and separate item rights in `research/resources.json`. Updated the bat evidence note, collection decision, model-data decision and researcher starter. Across the full 91,080-row annotation item—not the paper’s filtered 35,074 records—the report counts 48 known IDs, 70,001 rows with two identified participants, 136 distinct pairs, three connected components (largest 31 IDs), and 13 known IDs with no pair edge. `Annotations.csv` passed the existing exact-size, MD5 and SHA-256 receipt checks; the regression test confirms the report never emits sample subject or file identifiers.

**Limits and next decision.** The graph cannot verify the paper’s exact folds or whether its holdout unit is emitter, both participants, session, treatment, channel or chamber. The separate 31.6 MB FileInfo.csv item has no upstream MD5 and remains above the 5 MiB collection cap; it was not downloaded. A bat-bioacoustics reviewer should define the necessary generalization unit before any integrity-plan exception or further acquisition. No model was trained; no audio or video was acquired; the annotation table remains excluded from training. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 12 continuation: add a bounded Dupont's lark dataset review

Reviewed the full publisher version of Pérez-Granados et al. (2026), including recording and annotation methods, the data-record description, technical validation, usage notes and rights statement. It reports 4,297 songs from 191 males and 795 calls from 97 males across 20 Spanish populations. The event labels classify acoustic song/call types; they are not independent behavior labels or evidence of shared meaning. The study used one primary expert annotator, with additional review focused on uncertain or population-shared classifications; birds were unmarked, and song/call cohorts could not be linked. The authors caution against broad frequency-feature comparisons because vocal types vary spatially.

Added source records 194–195, a research note and a metadata-only dataset resource. The article itself is CC BY-NC-ND 4.0; the linked Zenodo record and its separately named R validation script could not be inspected, so their file inventory and licenses remain unresolved. No data, code, figures or article files were downloaded, copied or executed. Added the study to the reading path and atlas with one Spain-level marker; no individual sites or rounded threatened-species coordinates are exposed. It is a reference for event annotation and repertoire methods, not the current context-prediction benchmark. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

Keep the processing gate open and use individual URL Inspection plus internal links for priority pages. ## October 12 continuation: screen the upcoming Horizon biodiversity-monitoring call

Question: is there a substantial open call that could fund useful Talk2Nature work without overstating current readiness? The European Research Executive Agency's official call page says HORIZON-NATURE-2027-02 opens October 13, with a January 14, 2027 deadline at 17:00 Brussels time. The Commission's 2026–2027 Horizontal Activities work programme identifies topic HORIZON-NATURE-2027-02-05, “Cost-effective biodiversity monitoring for EU policies,” as a €16 million Research and Innovation Action, expecting three projects at €5–6 million each, lump sum. Its work is limited to policy-linked pollinator monitoring, soil biodiversity or Water Framework Directive monitoring. It calls for scalable methods, demonstration cases, species identification/quantification, advanced methods including genomic approaches, and FAIR-by-design data. The REA says most Horizon proposals require three partner organisations in three EU or associated countries, at least one from the EU; topic-specific rules control.

Added official source records 201–203, a public opportunity card, an honest fit assessment and a private application-register watch entry. Decision: no standalone application. The app has no validated species identification/counting or operational policy-monitoring evidence, and there is no confirmed applicant, scientific lead or consortium. Directly reviewed the public CSIC README and archive response headers. An October 12 follow-up to the official Zenodo API resolved the DOI difference: concept DOI `17264916` redirects to version record/DOI `17264917`, whose `conceptdoi` field is `17264916`. The record-level license is CC BY 4.0 and its eight-file inventory supplies sizes and MD5 values; these are provider metadata and have not been locally verified. PR [#28](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/28) merged as `4f01fc186dee506eeaf4c740f078371c1ebb2864`; manual Pages run [#38277855444](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38277855444) passed tests, public build, SEO/link and publication-boundary checks, and deployment. ## October 13 research update — Edmond terms before parrot-data retrieval

Rechecked the official Edmond v1 API for `10.17617/3.RUIM5I`: version 1, released, dataset-level MIT, three ZIP archives totaling 100,498,691,932 bytes, with provider-supplied MD5 values. The version metadata reports `fileAccessRequest: true`; file-level access was not resolved. Reviewed current Edmond access/download documentation, ZIP member viewer, Terms of Use and the official notice that the terms took effect July 20, 2026. The terms require acceptance before download; published deposits are retained at least ten years and cannot be deleted by their submitter except through exceptional deaccession at Edmond's discretion. The repository service is limited to Max Planck Society-associated research projects and scientific partners.

No terms were accepted and no archive/member was downloaded. The sample remains static-only, outside training and redistribution. Do not use Edmond as the participant-intake database or a withdrawable private working store. A later retrieval requires a justified single-file question, privacy/provenance review and explicit approval to accept the terms. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**. Sources 57, 219–220 and 225–226; resource `monk-parakeet-annotations`.

The public build produces 91 pages plus 404; `check_site.py` validates 92 HTML files and 4,306 links, with 91 reachable public pages. The research note visibly links the two policy sources, has its expected canonical URL, and appears in the sitemap. Search Console remains unverified: no Search Console connector was available in this audit, and the public site-restricted search query returned no results on the search surface used. That is not evidence that Google has not indexed the pages.

## October 13 continuation — reuse cross-species dataset infrastructure

**Question:** can Talk2Nature build on an existing cross-taxon data-access tool instead of duplicating loaders, while keeping scientific and legal boundaries intact?

**Evidence reviewed:** the Earth Species Project `alp-data` README and project configuration, plus exact PyPI release metadata for `alp-data` 1.11.1. The README describes a shared Python interface to 35+ bioacoustic datasets across taxa, streaming/transforms and per-dataset metadata. PyPI lists version 1.11.1 as released July 31, 2026, under MIT, with Python >=3.11. The project itself says each dataset retains its original terms. The repository overview is mutable; no specific dataset entry or underlying file was inspected.

**Change:** added source records 233–234, a source-linked research note, a metadata-only code resource, and a link in the model/evaluation learning path. The note recommends treating `alp-data` as a discovery and optional adapter candidate, not as a uniform data license or evidence of animal meaning. No package, dependencies, datasets or recordings were downloaded, installed or executed; no integration was added.

**Validation:** 94 Python tests and 105 Node tests pass; the resource catalog validates 70 records. The local noindex build generated 93 pages plus the custom 404 and passed the site checker (94 HTML files, 4,332 links). The public-origin build for the verified GitHub Pages URL passed (94 HTML files, 4,426 links, 93 reachable public pages); the new page is canonical/indexable in that local build and included in its sitemap. These checks are local and do not constitute deployment or Google indexing.

**Limits and next decision:** the aggregator's MIT license covers its code only. Dataset-specific rights, consent, provenance, labels and animal/session splits remain unreviewed. Before adding a dependency or acquiring data, choose one narrowly defined question and inspect one exact dataset release end-to-end. The integrated project goal remains active across the research library, mobile app, open-source health, SEO and benchmark-readiness tracks.

## October 13 continuation — parrot detection evidence and model boundary

**Question:** is a useful first ML step for the Field Companion acoustic event detection, and does existing parrot work support translation claims?

**Evidence reviewed:** selected sections of the peer-reviewed ANIMAL-SPOT paper (abstract, task/data methods, Tables 1 and 3, and relevant results/discussion), official repository README/license/split guidance, and MPG.PuRe article/dataset metadata. The paper reports distinct target/noise and monk-parakeet call-type tasks; the latter covers alarm/contact/other plus noise. It reports 92.7% accuracy on human-labeled test samples and 88.4% on machine-selected segments. The repository describes grouping excerpts from the same year/tape when creating splits. Individual/animal grouping and the exact data archive were not verified.

**Decision and change:** added the research note `animal-spot-parrot-detection-2022`, linked it in the detection and model/evaluation learning paths, and added separate metadata-only entries for the CC BY 4.0 article, GPL-3.0 code and unresolved Edmond dataset. “Animal-independent” here means a reusable framework assessed with species-specific data/configurations; it is not a cross-species animal-language model. The practical app lesson is to expose a sound/call category only as a reviewable hypothesis and keep it separate from observed context. No app classifier was added.

**Checks:** all 94 Python and 105 Node tests pass; the resource catalog validates 73 records. Local noindex build: 94 pages plus 404, site checker 95 HTML documents / 4,387 links. Local public-origin build: same 94 pages plus 404, 95 HTML documents / 4,482 links, 94 reachable public pages. The new page is emitted in the sitemap with its canonical/indexable metadata. These are local publication checks only.

**Rights and limits:** no article, code, model, recording, split or dataset file was copied or executed. MPG.PuRe identifies the article as CC BY 4.0; the repository declares GPL-3.0; the dataset deposit's exact inventory and terms remain unverified. The paper's results are not reproduced, individual-held-out performance is not established, phone microphone behavior is not tested, and no animal meaning is inferred. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**Next decision:** verify the exact Edmond Version 2 manifest, data terms, annotation provenance and animal/session grouping through an allowed route; obtain qualified parrot/bioacoustics review before considering a bounded detector benchmark. Do not add ANIMAL-SPOT as a runtime dependency or admit its data before those gates pass.

## October 13 ANIMAL-SPOT dataset metadata audit

**Question:** what is in the exact Edmond release referenced by ANIMAL-SPOT, and what would it support as a first benchmark?

**Evidence reviewed:** inspected Edmond's V2.0 landing page and metadata API, the official Edmond download/license/versioning guide, and the ANIMAL-SPOT repository README and split guidance. Edmond V2.0 was published 2024-04-08 and lists one public 522.0 MB `ANIMAL-SPOT_FILES.tar.gz` archive with MD5 `5443c6564c377d19576f5ea74b2bd061` under a dataset-level CC BY 4.0 declaration. Edmond says downloaders must accept its terms and the dataset license; no terms were accepted and no file was downloaded. The linked full Terms of Use page was blocked by Edmond's anti-bot and was not reviewed. The repository README describes 6,745 monk-parakeet target/noise excerpts and the supplied split, while the framework guide groups samples by year+tape. These repository descriptions and split files have not been checked inside the V2 archive.

**Decision and change:** corrected the resource record to V2.0, recorded the exact public archive metadata and its download condition, added an Edmond policy source, and narrowed this deposit's candidate task to target/noise detection. The V2 deposit is not documented as the separate call-type corpus in the paper. Year+tape grouping reduces same-tape leakage but does not establish individual-animal holdout if an animal appears on multiple tapes. Keep the dataset metadata-only; do not treat it as a ready benchmark or imply its labels, split or component rights were independently inspected.

**Checks and limits:** all 95 Python and 105 Node tests pass; all three edited JSON files parse, and the metadata-only catalog validates 73 resources. No audio, labels, split files, model weights or code were acquired or executed. No dataset terms were reviewed or accepted; no data were admitted to training. This is a metadata and README review, not a content audit or qualified scientific review.

## October 13 continuation — full-paper review sharpens the ANIMAL-SPOT benchmark question

**Question:** what does the primary paper actually support about held-out performance, and what can the Edmond monk-parakeet deposit test?

**Evidence reviewed:** read the complete publisher article text, including methods for preprocessing and data partitioning, the target/noise and call-type experiments, results, discussion, and ethics/data-collection description; revisited Tables 1 and 3 and the official ANIMAL-SPOT split guidance. The paper says samples from any one recording stay in a single partition, with a default 70/15/15 train/validation/test split. It reports mean test-set accuracy of 97.9% (range 94.5–99.8%) and, separately, 95.9% average AUC (range 91.7–99.1%) across 33 additional unseen animal-specific recordings. Monk-parakeet call-type accuracy is 92.7% on human-labeled test examples and 88.4% on detector-selected segments. The paper does not establish that individual animals are held out. The supplement's split tables and exact Edmond archive remain uninspected.

**Decision and change:** updated the source note and resource ledger to separate recording-held-out evaluation from animal-held-out generalization. The only defensible candidate is a reproduction of monk-parakeet target/noise performance on the exact V2.0 deposit and its recording-level split, reported per held-out recording. An individual-held-out claim is a separate question, possible only if the archive has suitable identity metadata and a specialist approves the grouping. This remains a proposal, not an approved run; Edmond terms were not accepted, no data or code was acquired or executed, and AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

**SEO correction:** the older goal text incorrectly called the already-published ANIMAL-SPOT note undeployed. Before this full-text update, live HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for the note, sitemap and robots, and the note appeared in the sitemap. At this candidate-audit stage the updated wording was pending release; the release receipt below records the superseding deployment. Search Console remains unrefreshed, so the live checks confirm delivery/crawl configuration but not Google indexing. The stale statement was corrected; no Search Console data or ranking claim was invented.

**Checks and limits:** all 96 Python tests and 105 Node tests passed; the metadata-only catalog validates 73 records. Local noindex build: 94 pages plus 404, 95 HTML files and 4,393 links. Public-origin build: 94 pages plus 404, 95 HTML files, 4,488 links and 94 reachable public pages. The private workbench's catalog sync imported only curated public metadata: 372 records total, 20 created on the first run and 0 changes on the repeat. All three edited JSON files parse and `git diff --check` passes. Before this edit, live checks confirmed the published note, sitemap and robots were available and the note was listed in the sitemap; the updated wording is local pending release. These checks verify the local build and prior crawl availability, not indexing or scientific reproduction. No model or dataset was acquired or run; Edmond terms were not accepted. Physical-phone and independent specialist review remain open.

## October 13 biodiversity-format mapping gate

**Question:** can the current Station journal be translated into a biodiversity-data package without implying a known organism or a scientifically defined survey? The official [Darwin Core Data Package guide](https://dwc.tdwg.org/dp/) requires a versioned profile, at least one table, and field descriptors taken from the official per-table schemas. The exact versioned profile and schema-index paths linked by the guide returned HTTP 404 in this environment. The official [TDWG source repository snapshot](https://github.com/tdwg/rs.tdwg.org/tree/e0cc2509953eeed24ad312b6e91e7bfcadd37a3b/dwc-dp) contains an unversioned profile and tables; the profile and event schema trace to commit `e0cc2509953eeed24ad312b6e91e7bfcadd37a3b`, whose message is “Initial pre-review DwC-DP schemas.” It is not treated as the released 1.0 artifact. The public-review index's status for [issue #759](https://github.com/tdwg/dwc/issues/759) was stale: direct GitHub state shows the issue closed on June 25, 2026, and its visible comments point to guide section 3.4 as already stating the field-descriptor rule.

**Decision:** defer the adapter. The current Station session is an operational capture session, not a protocol-defined sampling event; device time is unverified, browser location is absent, and observer-selected animal context is not an organism identification. No schema files, datasets or media were stored; no adapter or export was implemented. Next, obtain the exact released versioned schemas and define a qualified study protocol, then make a synthetic-only fixture if both gates pass. Details are in [`DATA_INTEROPERABILITY.md`](DATA_INTEROPERABILITY.md), source 259 and resource `darwin-core-dp-prereview-schema-snapshot`. This is a documentation/catalog update; app behavior, data intake and deployment are unchanged.

## October 13 live Station mobile-touch rehearsal

**Question:** does the optional whole-session setting note work through touch input at narrow phone widths on the deployed site? **Procedure:** loaded the public Station route in desktop Chrome using Playwright mobile emulation (`is_mobile` and `has_touch`) at 320 × 740 and 390 × 844 CSS pixels. Started the explicit no-microphone synthetic rehearsal, waited for the page to report “Synthetic session,” stopped it, and tapped the 48-pixel “Other animals” label with Playwright's touchscreen API. Event listeners confirmed `pointerType: touch` and `touchstart`; the first tap selected the category and the second cleared it.

**Result and limits:** at both widths, body/document widths matched the viewport, with no horizontal overflow or JavaScript page errors. A guarded `getUserMedia` stub saw zero calls, and the browser made zero non-read network requests. Only an in-memory synthetic session was used; it was not exported or uploaded. This closes the mobile-emulated tap gap for this control on Chromium, not physical-device/iOS Safari, screen-reader or first-time-user testing. No code change was needed; see [`MOBILE_AUDIT.md`](MOBILE_AUDIT.md). The broader program goal remains active.

## October 13 Station browser download and Compare round-trip

**Question:** does Save session produce a ZIP the app can reopen, and does a whole-session observer note survive without implying that zero retained clips means silence? **Procedure:** served the current local noindex build and used desktop Chromium at a 390 × 844 CSS-pixel mobile viewport. The browser harness enabled download capture and replaced `getUserMedia` with a counted rejection. It ran the invented synthetic path only, stopped it, selected “Other animals” in the optional setting-note control, captured the ZIP download to a temporary directory, opened the archive, acknowledged the save, navigated to Compare and loaded the same file into slot A.

**Result:** Station and Compare returned HTTP 200. The browser generated a nonempty ZIP containing the session JSON and `READ-ME.txt`; no WAV was expected because the synthetic session retained zero sound clips. Compare showed the preserved setting as “Other animals · observer-entered,” zero retained recordings, zero observations and “No audio was retained. That does not establish silence.” The body/document width was 390 pixels, with zero microphone calls and zero page errors. The temporary file was automatically deleted after the check.

**Limits and next decision:** this confirms browser download bytes and the local app's import path; it does not confirm the user's default Downloads folder or a native file chooser. It is Chromium viewport emulation, not a physical phone, iOS Safari, accessibility or first-time-user study. No implementation change, external data, real recording, animal exposure or participant intake occurred. Record the workflow as locally verified; retain physical-device and independent novice checks as open release evidence. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**.

## October 13 HyenaSET dataset and benchmark review — published

**Question:** does a new, large animal-vocalization dataset offer a defensible first benchmark, or a useful methods reference? Reviewed the targeted data-collection, annotation, split, benchmark and availability sections in the bioRxiv v2 preprint, plus official Edmond/DataCite metadata for dataset v1. The record lists 590,480 ten-second clips (about 1,640 hours) from 19 spotted hyenas, including 87,394 expert-reviewed clips (about 243 hours). It describes five sample-stratified splits and a four-individual holdout. The dataset-specific terms are CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, and its single archive is 107,184,528,475 bytes with a provider MD5 not locally verified.

**Scientific decision:** keep HyenaSET as a detection/call-label and split-design comparator, not a Talk2Nature communication or context benchmark. The preprint is not peer-reviewed; its event/source labels do not establish receiver response or message meaning. It says animal2vec pretraining uses all available audio before fine-tuning on the first split. We flag that as a transductive-evaluation concern unless the authors clarify otherwise; this is our methods interpretation. Dataset terms exclude it from the shared commercial-capable model path. The official data description also maps randomized names to identities and recording times for four test individuals; no rows or precise locations were inspected, and the atlas does not publish a map marker pending a wildlife-location review.

**Change:** added one source-linked research note, three source records and separate preprint/dataset resource records. Linked the note from the event-detection reading path. The catalog now has 74 research notes, 297 source records and 122 resources. No data, code or weights were acquired, installed, executed or admitted to training.
