## October 14 open-source dependency maintenance — merged, rescan pending

The authenticated GitHub Community Profile endpoint reported 100% community-health completeness. A separate repository-security check found vulnerability alerts disabled; they are now enabled. The first alert query returned none, but GitHub's scan after the branch push identified two open moderate findings for `requests==2.32.3` in `admin/requirements.txt`: [CVE-2024-47081](https://github.com/psf/requests/security/advisories/GHSA-9hjg-9r4m-mvj7), patched in 2.32.4, and [CVE-2026-25645](https://github.com/psf/requests/security/advisories/GHSA-gc5v-m9x4-r6x2), patched in 2.33.0. The latter only affects applications that call `extract_zipped_paths()` directly; standard Requests usage is not affected. The upstream [Requests 2.34.2 release](https://pypi.org/project/requests/2.34.2/) is available. PR [#124](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/124) merged to `main` as `4a1981bd8d6a8521b795e8c0a9e9d06bd448c235`; `admin/requirements.txt` and the runtime `admin/requirements.lock` now both pin 2.34.2. Immediately after merge, the GitHub API still listed both alerts as open, with `updated_at` timestamps before the merge; a post-merge dependency rescan has not yet been reflected.

Dependabot security-fix PRs remain disabled. The private service is built from `admin/requirements.lock`, a custom filename, while GitHub's [supported pip manifests](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/reference/supply-chain-security/dependency-graph-supported-package-ecosystems) include `requirements.txt` and `Pipfile.lock`; automating pip fixes before a supported, reproducible lock update path exists could update the source requirements without updating the deployed lock. No private dependency manifest beyond the synchronized `requests` pin was changed. A new unit test checks that every direct dependency pin matches its lock entry.

Added `.github/dependabot.yml` for weekly GitHub Actions and Docker version checks, limited to three and two open update PRs respectively. GitHub's [Actions update guide](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/how-tos/secure-your-supply-chain/secure-your-dependencies/auto-update-actions) documents this setup and the resulting reviewable update PRs; no automatic merges are configured. No pip updates are configured. The active workflow refs are pinned to commit SHAs and the Docker base image to a digest, so candidate updates must continue to be tested before merging. At the time of this checkpoint, the first Dependabot version-update PRs had not yet appeared; see the dated follow-up below.

## October 14 parrot video-call Table 2 arithmetic review

**Question:** does the apparent inconsistency in the published parrot call-count table come from text extraction, or is it visible in the rendered paper? Retrieved the official University of Glasgow published PDF, rendered page 9 and read Table 2 alongside its displayed session counts. Checked the current Glasgow repository record and the DOI's Crossref relation metadata. The repository record lists the paper as CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 and states that study data are unavailable; Crossref's relation object is empty. No correction was found in the checked records, but this check is not exhaustive.

**Finding:** the displayed session counts sum to 65 live calls and 40 playback calls out of 108 possible per phase. Recalculating count-based live-minus-playback percentage-point differences for P1–P9 gives +50, +17, +75, +17, +8, -8, -8, +33 and +25. The paper's row labelled “Live minus playback” prints the reverse sign for P1, P2, P4 and P6–P9; P3/P5 also appear transposed. This is an arithmetic check of the visible table, not an author correction or raw-data reanalysis. The separate Table 3 duration discrepancy and reported inferential tests remain unresolved.

**Change and limits:** updated source record 49 and the public research note with the visual check and its limits. No raw data, recordings, supplementary files or code were acquired; no author was contacted. Preserve the publication's reported figures separately from this arithmetic audit. Do not use participant-level table values or adapt the study protocol until qualified avian/animal-computer-interaction and statistical review; any author contact requires separate authorization.

## October 14 funding follow-up — BiodivFuture national submission route

**Question:** what does an Israeli research partner need to do in addition to the international BiodivFuture pre-proposal?

**Evidence reviewed:** the latest official Biodiversa+ Funding Organisation Rules V5 (6 October, Israel/MoEP pages 56–57), the official MoEP Israel briefing slides, and Israel's Government Procurement Administration notice for tender 2026/4. The international call owner and formal call document give the mandatory EPSS deadline as November 10, 2026 at 12:00 CET; the European Commission listing still shows November 2. Israel's separate tender lists an October 22 opening and November 15 deadline at 12:00. The MoEP briefing requires two submissions (EPSS and the Ministry's digital tender box), a one-page Hebrew summary describing the Israeli work, and matching information in both applications. V5 requires an Israel Science Foundation-recognized host institution; independent researchers, NGOs and similar contributors may participate through a qualifying host, and part of the project must take place in Israel. The rules list €120,000 as the Israeli contribution and per-project maximum, and generally do not allow adding a new partner after Step 1 except under an additional call.

**Change and decision:** added the current V5 rules, national tender record and MoEP briefing as source records 309–311. The public opportunity entry now distinguishes the international and Israeli deadlines and links directly to the national documents. Talk2Nature remains a possible methods contributor only inside a defined, ecology-led transnational study of a novel ecosystem; no eligible host, site, field dataset or consortium is confirmed. This is not a standalone software grant. No application, partner profile, contact or outreach was made. The tender attachments were not retrievable in this review, so the complete national application package remains unreviewed.

**Validation:** all 125 repository Python tests passed. The local noindex build produced 107 pages plus 404; `scripts/check_site.py` validated 108 HTML documents and 5,263 links. The explicit-origin public candidate also produced 107 pages plus 404; the checker validated 108 HTML documents, 5,372 links and all 107 public pages reachable. The focused regression verifies the two separate dates, source links, eligibility caveats and non-submission status. The public candidate has not yet been deployed; Search Console indexing and external applicant eligibility remain unverified.

**Release:** PR [#150](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/150) merged to `main` as `3b1b980734e1d53c3343ab9251f5f7878760ff19`. Manual [Pages run #38501898231](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38501898231) passed tests, the checked public build, link/SEO/boundary checks and deployment.

Live HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for the funding page, sitemap and `robots.txt`. ## October 14 ANIMAL-SPOT dataset access-terms review

**Question:** what do the current Edmond terms require before Talk2Nature could inspect the public monk-parakeet ANIMAL-SPOT V2.0 archive?

**Evidence reviewed:** Edmond's official Terms of Use and Data Access & Usage guide, rechecked October 14, 2026, alongside the existing exact-version V2.0 metadata. The guide says public datasets are accessible without an account but downloading requires acceptance of Edmond's terms and the dataset-specific license. The Terms say published datasets are retained for at least ten years and normally cannot be erased by submitters, with exceptional deaccession decided by Edmond. Keep V2.0 `metadata_only`; do not accept terms or download without explicit owner approval. ## October 14 — budgerigar playback-context evidence note

**Question:** can playback change parrot vocal output, and what does that imply for Talk2Nature's passive phone workflow?

**Evidence reviewed:** Seki and Dooling (2016), *Effect of auditory stimuli on conditioned vocal behavior of budgerigars* (DOI 10.1016/j.beproc.2015.11.002). Reviewed the PubMed citation/abstract and selected PMC author-manuscript sections surfaced by the article index: subjects, apparatus, stimuli, procedures, analyses, and results/discussion. The listed supplement, raw data and code were not inspected. No reanalysis, replication or independent review was performed.

**Finding and boundary:** five individually housed budgerigars (one male, four females) were operantly trained to call after a visual cue. Calls in the call-plus-flock condition were louder than in the comparison conditions; only three birds produced the non-primary calls used in the call-variation result. This is evidence that acoustic context can alter conditioned vocal output in this setup. It does not test message meaning, receiver comprehension, consumer apps or ordinary household behavior. The small sample, fixed condition sequence and training limit generalization. The paper reports an institutional animal-care approval and a restricted-weight protocol; that protocol is not consumer-app guidance. Keep public playback disabled.

**Change and rights:** added research note `budgerigar-playback-context`, source 312, metadata-only resource `budgerigar-conditioned-vocal-output-seki-dooling-2016`, and a link from the meaning-learning path. Article reuse terms and data/code rights remain unverified; no article, supplement, data, audio or code was acquired.

**Validation:** all 126 Python tests and 109 Node tests passed; the catalog accepted 131 resources. The local noindex build produced 108 pages plus 404; the checker validated 109 HTML documents and 5,308 links. The explicit-origin public candidate also produced 108 pages plus 404; the checker validated 109 HTML documents, 5,417 links and all 108 public pages reachable. The route has a unique title/description, `index,follow`, a self-canonical URL and a sitemap entry.

**Publication receipt:** PR [#155](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/155) merged to `main` as [`08e3dc8`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/08e3dc844088d0cb7ce5de6557c0d62794cddca3). Public checks [#38506675389](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38506675389) passed, including native Android checks. Manual Pages run [#38506845696](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38506845696) passed build and deployment. Live HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for the research page, sitemap and `robots.txt`; the page has its self-canonical and `index,follow`, appears in the sitemap, and `robots.txt` names that sitemap. ## October 14 — honeybee vision and the camera-app boundary

Added a source-linked note on Wario et al. (2017), plus a pinned GitHub code/data review and a separate 2015 BeesBook engineering comparator. The 2017 system measures a known honeybee waggle-run signal in a high-speed infrared observation hive and maps it against a calibrated feeder. Its 90.07% figure is validation accuracy from one colony, not a general gesture or phone-camera result. Distance requires known-location calibration. GitHub reports no recognized license for the pinned code/data repository, and the inspected tree contains no LICENSE/COPYING file.

Added four component-specific resource records with separate rights decisions: the 2017 article, code repository, ground-truth sample and 2015 system paper. Code and data remain metadata-only; no third-party files were downloaded or executed. The multimodal reading path now points to a concrete camera question and a public independent-review brief. No camera feature, animal-facing interaction or study result is claimed for the Field Companion.

Visitors review a report on the page, then choose to copy it or open a prefilled email draft. Nothing is sent or persisted by the form; the visitor must choose an external action. A long note hides the email link and leaves copy available. The contribution guide points to the helper. It collects software-workflow feedback only, not research observations.

The email URL was inspected but not opened or sent.

## October 14 — budgerigar caller-identity methods review

**Question:** what does the commonly cited budgerigar neural-pathway paper establish about call identity, call response and meaning, and does its classifier evaluation support an unseen-bird benchmark?

**Evidence reviewed:** Zhao et al. (2023), *Anterior forebrain pathway in parrots is necessary for producing learned vocalizations with individual signatures*, *Current Biology* 33(24):5415–5426.e4, DOI 10.1016/j.cub.2023.11.014, PMCID PMC10799565. Reviewed the full PMC text through NCBI E-utilities, including behavior paradigm, VAE/SVM methods, results, discussion, animal-care and data/code statements. Cross-checked the article's Mendeley v1 reference against the existing versioned resource and previous checksum-pinned one-file inspection. No new article/data file was retained; no code/model was run, no reanalysis or independent review was performed.

**Finding:** eight budgerigars in the contact-call cohort produced 180–1,612 calls per bird per day in response to colony-noise playback. A VAE plus SVM classified caller identity above 90% in a balanced, random 80/20 call-level split repeated 50 times; accuracy stayed around 90% for two call types shared across birds. Cross-time prediction from week 3 to week 15 dropped from about 90% to 40%. In a separate five-bird experiment, birds continued responding to individual calls during AFP inactivation, though response rate fell in three birds and latency changes varied. The neural intervention also altered call acoustics and reduced individual discriminability. The study supports acoustic signatures and a production mechanism in its laboratory setup; it does not decode call meaning.

**Evaluation, rights and decision:** the identity split holds birds in both train and test data, so the score does not establish performance on unseen birds, sites, devices or species. The work used captive birds, controlled loudspeaker playback, specialized microphones and invasive neural procedures. The paper's PMC notice permits text mining/fair-use use but is not an open redistribution license; its Mendeley data deposit has a separate CC BY 4.0 declaration, while custom scripts are available from the lead contact without a verified public license. Updated the existing research note/source and added a separate paper resource record. Keep the existing one-file sample exploratory: sample rate `44101` and file-to-caller/session/condition mapping remain unresolved. AutoResearch remains **NOT READY**; do not use this study as an unseen-bird or call-meaning benchmark.

**Verification:** 140 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed; the source catalog validates 141 resources. The explicit-origin public build produced 110 pages plus 404, and the site checker validated 111 HTML files, 5,538 links and all 110 public routes. The new evidence page has its canonical URL and `index,follow` metadata, links to the PMC article, and visibly states the held-out-bird limitation; the research-resource directory includes the paper record. These are build and crawl checks, not live deployment or search-engine indexing evidence.

**Release receipt:** PR [#170](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/170) passed public checks [#38525514239](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38525514239) and merged to `main` as [`124d8cd`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/124d8cd00ca516b3ce4e35f239a14de12596eeda). Manual [Pages run #38525802449](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38525802449) passed both its public build and live-site verifier. Separate HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for the evidence page, resource directory, sitemap and robots file; confirmed the page's canonical/indexable metadata and primary article link, its sitemap entry and resource-directory listing, and the robots sitemap directive. This verifies technical publication and crawl configuration, not Search Console discovery, indexing or rankings.

## October 14 — budgerigar deposit structure and sample-provenance follow-up

**Question:** does the versioned data deposit connect the inspected MAT sample to the study's contact-call cohort, exact caller/session/condition, or acquisition sample rate?

**Evidence reviewed:** the official [Mendeley Data v1 landing page](https://data.mendeley.com/datasets/j8rpy4dc6c/1) and [API documentation](https://data.mendeley.com/api/docs/), the paper's full PMC methods and Figure 2 description, the existing local sample report, and the current ignored local sample. The official landing page says the deposit contains MATLAB struct arrays including raw audio, assigns Figure 2 files to `ColonyNoiseCalls`, and assigns Figures 3–5 to `acoustiSAT_MAO.mat`. The article identifies Figure 2 as contact-call individual signatures from eight budgerigars. The existing sample report identifies the selected 1.49 MB file as a smallest-file item from `ColonyNoiseCalls`; a standard-library SHA-256 check reconfirmed its 1,486,634-byte size and pinned digest. The sample was not parsed or played again.

**Access and result:** the documented public file-list endpoint uses OAuth2; an unauthenticated request for version 1 returned HTTP 401. No current file manifest was obtained. The landing-page organization narrows the sample to the Figure 2 collection, but does not identify its bird, session or experimental condition. The reviewed article methods do not state the digitization rate, so the MAT field `fs=44101` remains unexplained. No caller or treatment mapping, independent behavior label, rate correction or baseline is claimed.

**Decision:** added the official API documentation as a separate source and updated the paper, dataset and methods note with their distinct scopes. Keep the acquired sample local for structure inspection only and `training_admitted: false`. Next, seek a publicly accessible or authorized per-file manifest and independent avian-bioacoustics/statistical review before any benchmark. No author contact, additional file acquisition, audio playback or model execution occurred.

**Validation:** all 141 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed; the resource catalog validates 141 records. The new site regression checks the figure-level folders, unresolved sample identity and API access limit. The local noindex build produced 110 pages plus 404; the checker validated 111 HTML documents and 5,431 links. The explicit-origin public candidate produced 110 pages plus 404; the checker validated 111 HTML documents, 5,542 links and all 110 public routes. `git diff --check` is clean. This is a checked candidate pending PR and release; no live publication is claimed here.

**Release receipt:** PR [#172](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/172) passed public checks [#38543480426](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38543480426) and merged to `main` as [`971df0b`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/971df0b5ce6b94b22f2e2526f4b5b77e64c010c5). Manual [Pages run #38543743905](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/actions/runs/38543743905) passed its public build and live-site verifier. Separate HTTPS checks returned HTTP 200 for the research page, resource directory, sitemap and robots file; verified canonical/indexable metadata, source citations, figure-folder context, unresolved sample mapping/rate, training boundary and sitemap/robots links. This confirms technical publication and crawl configuration, not search-engine discovery, indexing or rankings.

## October 14 — yellow-naped Amazon cultural-change record

**Question:** does the public dataset and associated code provide a rights-clear, reproducible audio benchmark for parrot communication?

**Evidence reviewed:** Dahlin et al. (2024) abstract and targeted Methods/Results passages surfaced by the PMC index; the full PMC page presented a bot check and direct publisher retrieval returned HTTP 403. Reviewed Dryad v6 landing-page/data-dictionary sections, Dryad's current CC0 policy, and the TLS-verified API record/version/file manifest. Dryad reports seven files totaling 9,970,731 bytes, including 2,461 call-level measurement rows and 56 site-year rows; the README says no restrictions on deposited data, but raw audio and roost counts are not in the archive and exact site coordinates are withheld. The version API provides SHA-256 values. The separate analysis repository is MIT at commit 1399b7a2de6354d27b5227bb75de7e285fe3d636; static review of its four R Markdown analyses found author-specific absolute paths, an unpinned latest warbleR install, archived rgdal/rgeos setup notes and missing inputs. No code was run.

**Decision and change:** added original research note yellow-naped-amazon-vocal-culture-2024, three component-specific resource records (article, Dryad dataset, GitHub code), and separate paper/data/API/policy/code source records. The note distinguishes dialect change from meaning, records within-site-year caller IDs and the group-split risk, and links the observation period to the existing country-scale Costa Rica atlas point. Article text remains all rights reserved; Dryad data are CC0; code is MIT. The paper review is not full text. Dryad API file download returned 401 and its public file-stream endpoint returned 403; no file bytes, exact site names, audio, model or participant information were collected.

**Limit and next decision:** this is a useful historical and schema reference, not a reproducible audio or communication benchmark. Full article/supplement review and independent parrot-bioacoustics/statistical assessment remain necessary before a baseline; any future file review should use the documented Dryad route and retain training_admitted=false.

**Validation:** all 142 repository Python tests pass and the resource catalog validates 144 records. The local noindex build contains 111 pages plus 404; the checker verified 112 HTML documents and 5,494 links. The explicit-origin public candidate passed with 111 reachable public pages; 112 HTML documents and 5,606 links were checked. The new page has a unique 147-character description, self-canonical, index/follow metadata, visible component-specific rights and review limits, and a sitemap entry. These candidate checks validate technical crawl setup, but do not establish Google indexing or rankings.

**Release receipt:** PR [#175](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/pull/175) passed the required repository, browser-tool, native Android and preview-site checks, then merged as [`c5dd9b6`](https://github.com/Metivity/talk2nature/commit/c5dd9b6b0d015891ada1627981609de07a51c812). ## October 14 — Field Companion mobile accessibility smoke audit

**Question:** do the core Field Companion routes remain navigable and expose accessible names in a narrow mobile browser, including with keyboard focus and the browser accessibility tree?

**Review:** built the current worktree as a local noindex site and inspected `/app/`, `/app/station/?mode=parrot`, `/app/review/`, `/app/compare/` and `/app/check/` in headless Chrome with mobile viewport emulation. Checked all five routes at 390 × 844 CSS pixels and the four primary workflow routes at 320 × 740. At both widths, the tested pages matched the viewport width with no horizontal overflow. The accessibility tree exposed named interactive controls and the expected banner, main, navigation and footer landmarks; the current app destination was marked with `aria-current="page"`. Keyboard Tab reached the visible “Skip to content” link with a 3 px focus outline; Enter moved focus to `main`.

**Decision:** no reproducible defect in this bounded audit required an app-code change. The main paths remain usable in narrow browser emulation; the next validation should cover physical iOS/Android devices, VoiceOver/TalkBack and first-time user comprehension before making broader accessibility or usability claims.

**Limit:** this is a Chrome DOM/accessibility-tree and keyboard smoke check, not a WCAG conformance audit, physical-device test, screen-reader session or participant study. It used no microphone, animal media, camera, network submission or participant data. No third-party source or dataset was added or inspected.


## October 14 — EmotionalCanines evidence note and narrow-screen correction

**Question:** what does a recent dog-bark classification paper actually validate, and does the new evidence page work on very narrow phones?

**Evidence and change:** reviewed the author-hosted ACM Multimedia paper's dataset, annotation, reliability, dog-separated split, results and limits; pinned the exact Hugging Face card revision and GitHub dataset commit. Added one original research note, three source records and separate paper/dataset resource records. The paper's arousal and valence labels were assigned by people from associated video context; this is not an animal-reported emotion or evidence of call meaning. The reported dog-separated test is distinguished from 10-fold results whose grouping was unclear. Dataset and paper declarations are recorded separately; original internet-video rights, consent and privacy were not traced. No data, labels, code or weights were acquired or run.

The styled 320-pixel browser check exposed horizontal overflow from long pinned hashes in the provenance list, which sits outside the main article container. Added narrow text wrapping to article prose and the source list.

**Validation:** 143 Python tests and 115 Node tests passed; the resource catalog validates 146 records. No physical-phone, screen-reader or Search Console result is claimed. The candidate remains unpublished pending PR and deployment verification.
